# Tasks

These rules apply to every task. The design decisions (D1–D28) are in `design.md`; the behaviour each task delivers
is in this change's `specs/` (each task's **Satisfies** line names the requirement headings). Paths without a module
prefix are under `modules/<module>/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/<module>/`; tests under `src/test/java/`.

- **Size and order.** The owner asked for one change instead of the research's sixteen, so the ~10-task norm
  (`AGENTS.md#how-work-is-planned`) applies per group, not per change. There are 17 numbered groups (design D1); each
  is one build step and a mergeable unit, and group 17 is the whole gate and the hand run. Groups run in the order of
  their numbers, except where a heading says a group may run beside another. Each group's heading names the groups it
  needs. Each task may depend only on the repository and on earlier tasks. The repository is green between groups.
  A task that removes a method, field or enum constant updates every caller in the same task (or the removal moves to
  the task where its last caller goes). A task that adds a constant to an `:api` enum or a variant to a sealed `:api`
  type updates every exhaustive `switch` over it in every module in the same task — for `JobEvent` these are
  `ui: state/RunSession.dispatch`, `app: cli/UnattendedRun` and `pipeline: RunSummaryLogger`.
  `## Workflow follow-up` at the end lists the work done after archive (the stale `## Purpose` paragraphs and
  scenario titles a delta cannot edit); it has no checkboxes.
- **Git.** Work on `feature/redesign-translation-pipeline--<n>-<step>`, for example
  `--5-provider-engines`, in the main checkout (worktrees made by the agent tool start from `master`). Squash-merge one
  commit into `feature/redesign-translation-pipeline`, then delete the task branch. A checked task is complete only
  when that merge has succeeded (`AGENTS.md#git`). Never `--no-verify`, never force-push, never merge into `master`.
- **Acceptance test first.** Each group's first task writes the group's acceptance test, runs it and records it
  **red**. The group is done when that test is green, the gate is green and the group's hand check is done. Until the
  acceptance test turns green, `scripts/test-focused.sh --full-module` counts as green when every test passes except
  the acceptance class and the other tests the group's first task recorded red by name. The group branch is not pushed
  before the group's gate is green (pre-push runs the gate).
- **Tests.**
  - Name tests `method_state_expected`. Put no `if`, `for` or `while` in a test body; use `@ParameterizedTest`.
  - Use `@TempDir` for files. Write expected values out instead of recomputing them (`.claude/rules/testing.md`).
  - Use WireMock at the HTTP seam whenever a request or reply changes. Stub both the Ollama-native (`/api/*`) and the
    OpenAI-compatible (`/v1/*`) dialects where both apply. No Mockito.
  - Persistence is proven by the contract tests over **both** adapters, with SQLite on a temp file and the schema
    applied.
  - UI tests run on JavaFX 26's headless platform. "Wired" is shown by behaviour: drive the view-model and assert the
    node changed; fire the control and assert the recording fake was called.
  - Fixtures and examples are book-neutral, with invented names and sentences. Real books never enter the repository.
- **Research data.** Some tasks copy data from the owner-local research folder
  (`.temporary_context/ResearchesResultsForTheAppPipelineImprovement/`, git-ignored) into the repository as their first
  step. Examples are name cases, quant tiers, declared languages and recorded server bodies. A copied file that holds
  prompt or book text is made book-neutral first. Each such task restates the rule it implements, so the folder is
  only a data source. When the folder is absent, the task stops and asks the owner.
- **Paired evals ("arms").** A task that runs an arm states:
  - the sets it uses and the model it screens on (`gemma4:e4b-mlx` first, `gemma4:26b-mlx` once at the end, before the
    loser is deleted; the tasks use the owner's MLX tags for the models design D1 calls `gemma4:e4b` and `gemma4:26b`);
  - the decision rule and the default if the arm does not win;
  - how the alternative is selected: only through the test-only `PromptVariants` binding (added in group 1) or a
    test-injector binding of row data or a start size, never through an environment variable or a setting that
    `src/main` reads (the eval test classes may read `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_VARIANTS` under `src/test` to choose the
    binding). The loser is deleted in the same group.

  The task records the result in `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` under a `#redesign-arms` heading. When the result changes a
  default, the task updates the requirement, design.md and this file in the same commit. Arms run single-flight,
  never two at once.
- **Stored shapes.** A task that changes a stored record shape updates the record fingerprint (`StoredShapes`), the
  in-memory adapter and the contract tests. It also adds a JSON round-trip test. The next launch sets the old
  database aside (ADR-0043). The group's hand check notes that projects, providers and settings were reset.
- **Specification edits.** Each group fixes the `docs/specification/**`, `.claude/rules/**` and ADR clauses that its
  own behaviour change makes wrong, in the same group. The **Docs** bullet of the group's last implementation task
  lists them.
- **Cadence.** Run `scripts/test-focused.sh` while coding and `scripts/test-focused.sh --full-module` before each
  commit. Each group ends with `./gradlew clean build check spotlessCheck`; paste its tail. Before pushing any change
  to a screen, a dialog or a UI word, run `scripts/ci-linux-ui.sh`. Never run two gates at once. A run much longer
  than the last baseline is hung: kill it and diagnose.
- **Logging is part of done** (`.claude/rules/logging.md`):
  - INFO for lifecycle, DEBUG for every working method's parameters and every branch taken.
  - TRACE for book text, prompts and replies; never a credential.
  - WARN for degraded, retried or refused outcomes.
- **Reuse first.** Extend or replace the named existing class. Never add a second class beside it for the same job.
  design.md D27 lists the reuse map.

## 1. eval-harness-v2 — The measuring tool every later group is judged by (needs no earlier group; groups 2, 3 and 4 start after it)

Builds the harness that design D24 describes: datasets that carry a hash manifest, seeded repeats, one record per case, a
version-2 report, a local judge, paired statistics and the baselines every later group is compared with. It is test code,
scripts, committed invented data and one test seam in `src/main` (1.5: the `PromptVariants` binding) whose defaults keep
today's behaviour exactly. No product behaviour and no stored shape changes, so the database is not reset.
"Arm 1" is the paired eval that decides whether the local judge may be trusted; it runs here so groups 2 onward can use it.

- [ ] 1.1 Write the group's acceptance test and see it red. A dry run with the `pseudo` provider must write a complete version-2 report whose dataset root hash equals the hash in the dataset's manifest. Every later task in this group serves this test and the baselines. → :pipeline (tests)
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24 (eval harness v2 and the arms)
  - **Depends on:** existing repository state
  - **Read:** `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/eval/` — `EvalOutput`, `EvalProvenance`, `MiniRunner`, `MiniReport`, `MiniEvalOfflineTest` (the pattern: the whole mini flow on `PseudoChatModel`); design D24 "Report v2"
  - **Change:** add `EvalDryRunTest` beside `MiniEvalOfflineTest`. It runs the mini book on `PseudoChatModel` into a `@TempDir` history through `EvalOutput`, reads the written `report.json` and checks it. No production class changes in this task.
  - **Test first:** `dryRun_pseudoProvider_writesCompleteVersionTwoReportWithManifestRootHash` (unit, no network). It asserts: `version` is `2`; `dataset.id` is `mini-en` and `dataset.pair` is `en-uk`; `dataset.rootHash` equals the `rootHash` field the test reads out of `eval/datasets/mini-en/manifest.json` (the test reads the manifest, it never recomputes the hash); the blocks `model`, `judge` (with `mode` equal to `none`, because the pseudo run has no judge), `repeats` (with `k` equal to `1` and `seeds` equal to `[1000]`), `noiseBand` (null at K = 1) and `comparison` (null without a baseline) exist; every case entry has `graders`, `time` and `tokens`, and a time figure the pseudo model does not report is `null`, not `0`.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests '*EvalDryRunTest'` fails because there is no `version` and no `eval/datasets/mini-en/manifest.json`; paste the failing lines.

- [ ] 1.2 Add `DatasetManifest`, its check test, a manifest for the existing `sequence` set, and the empty `detectors-ru-uk` set with its runner. A dataset is trusted only when its files still hash to what the manifest says, so a changed fixture can never move a score unnoticed. → :pipeline (tests)
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24 (datasets "versioned by hash"; `detectors-ru-uk` runner and empty manifest)
  - **Depends on:** 1.1
  - **Excludes:** the reshaped `mini-en` and the new `mini-ru` (1.3, 1.4); the detector cases themselves arrive in group 10
  - **Read:** `eval/sequence/manifest.json` (it is the generator's own data, not a hash manifest; leave it alone), `SequenceBookLibrary`, `SequenceFixture`, `EvalProvenance.promptHash` (the existing SHA-256 style), design D24 "Datasets"
  - **Change:** add package-private record `DatasetManifest(id, version, pair, ownerData, files:[{path, sha256}], rootHash)` in `eval/` with a loader for `eval/datasets/<id>/manifest.json` and `static DatasetManifest of(Path folder)` that hashes every listed file after normalising line ends to LF. The root hash is the SHA-256 of the lines `path<TAB>sha256<LF>` sorted by path. File paths are relative to the `eval/` resource folder, so a manifest can list files that sit elsewhere. Write `eval/datasets/sequence/manifest.json` listing `eval/sequence/book.md` and `eval/sequence/manifest.json` (`ownerData: false`, pair `en-uk`). Write `eval/datasets/detectors-ru-uk/manifest.json` and `cases.json` (an empty array) and `DetectorsRuUkTest`, a parameterised test with `@ParameterizedTest(allowZeroInvocations = true)` over `cases.json` that group 10 fills.
  - **Test first:** `DatasetManifestTest.of_inventedTwoFileFolder_hashesLfNormalisedFilesToTheWrittenRootHash` (unit, `@TempDir`; the expected root hash is a literal computed once with `sha256sum` in the shell); `of_fileWithCrlfLineEnds_hashesLikeTheLfFile`; `DatasetManifestCheckTest.manifest_everyDatasetFolder_hasFilesThatStillHashToTheirManifest` (parameterised over every `eval/datasets/*/manifest.json`; the manifest holds the expected value, so this recomputes the files, not the manifest); `manifest_fileMissing_failsNamingThePath`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG on each manifest loaded with its id and file count; WARN on a mismatch with the path.
  - **Done when:** the four tests are green and the `detectors-ru-uk` test reports zero invocations as allowed; `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests '*Dataset*'` pasted.

It is the first dataset that exercises residue, stress marks, genders in oblique forms and the weak spots of the ru→uk pair, and it carries every labelled case a later group's acceptance test reads. → :pipeline (tests)
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24 (datasets, graders); it also holds the labelled cases the later groups' acceptance tests are written against (groups 7, 10, 11 and 13), so no later group has to add a block to it

  - **Change:** write `eval/datasets/mini-ru/{book.json, expectations.json, gold-map.json, labels.json, manifest.json}`. Invented text only; ru→uk; two chapters; the first-person narrator is not given a gender in the brief. Block ids are `<chapter file>:<line>`, as the pipeline names segments. The blocks hold one of each (design D24's sixteen plus the gender-after-the-run line group 13 needs, seventeen in all): a chapter heading `4. Ночной перевал` (heading number; group 10's acceptance test); a first-person line whose past-tense verb shows a woman (`Я вернулась поздно.`); a name in an oblique form before its nominative (`— Позови Велемира, — сказала она.` before any `Велемир`), labelled `inflected-form-as-new-name` (group 7's acceptance test: `Велемира` must not become its own entry); a genitive that reads like a woman's nominative while the base name's own verb shows a man (`Ключ Велемира лежал на столе. Велемир вернулся поздно.`), labelled `genitive-with-own-gender` (group 7's acceptance test: the entry `Велемир` stays male; the `-а` of the genitive is no female evidence); a line that starts with a dash and must keep it; a one-line Russian reply (`Нет, не сегодня.`) that must not come back as a source line; words only Russian has (`что`, `это`, `только`); a stress mark in the source (`за́мок`); a double negation (`Никто не пришёл.`); ти/Ви in one dialogue; a glossary seed with a stress mark (`Але́ксей`); a paragraph of three speakers; a second chapter heading; a paragraph a proofread could wrongly rewrite by adding a source word, at id `ch02.md:14` (source `Мирена кивнула и сказала, что ключ у Тарна.`, reference `Мирена кивнула й сказала, що ключ у Тарна.`; group 13's acceptance case a); a line at `ch02.md:16` whose name the person gives another gender after the run (source `Ивар сказал, что вернётся.`, reference `Івар сказав, що повернеться.`; labelled `gender-after-run`; group 13's acceptance cases b and c); the weak word `да` as an interjection; a female surname in two forms. Add graders to `CheckKind`: hard `TARGET_ALPHABET` (no letter outside the target alphabet, for any pair), `FOREIGN_SCRIPT`, `NO_SOURCE_LINE` (the target is not the source line), `GLOSSARY_ONE_ENTRY` (one entry per lemma), `GLOSSARY_GENDER`, `ONE_SPELLING_PER_LEMMA`; soft `SOURCE_ONLY_WORDS` (the case author's literal list, never the production set), `DIALOGUE_DASH`, `VOCATIVE`, `NARRATOR_GENDER`; reuse `ADDRESS_SAME`. A grader reads stored results and the output text only.
  - **Test first:** `MiniScorerTest` rows with invented targets (`NO_SOURCE_LINE`: source `Нет, не сегодня.`, target `Ні, не сьогодні.` passes, `Нет, не сегодня.` fails; `SOURCE_ONLY_WORDS` with list `[что]`: `Вона сказала, что прийде.` fails, `Вона сказала, що прийде.` passes; `TARGET_ALPHABET`: `Він знайшов ключ.` passes, `Він знайшов ключы.` fails; `GLOSSARY_ONE_ENTRY`: entries `Велемир` and `Велемира` fail, `Велемир` alone passes). `MiniRuEvalOfflineTest.run_pseudoModel_decidesEverySeventeenBlocksWithNoError` (the pseudo run, `@TempDir`). `MiniBookTest.load_miniRu_hasSeventeenBlocksInTwoChapters`; `MiniRuLabelsTest.labels_laterGroupCases_arePresent` (parameterised over `inflected-form-as-new-name`, `genitive-with-own-gender`, `gender-after-run` and the ids `ch02.md:14`, `ch02.md:16`).
  - **Done when:** the offline tests and the manifest check are green on both mini datasets.

In `eval-matrix.sh`: `--repeats K`, `--judge`, `--pair en-uk|ru-uk` and `--baseline-of SHA` (passed as `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_REPEATS`, `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_JUDGE`, `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_PAIR`, `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_BASELINE_OF`, read only by the eval test classes); for an Ollama run the script reads the quantisation from `/api/show` (`details.quantization_level`) and passes it as `BOOKLOOM_EVAL_QUANT`, for LM Studio from `/api/v0/models`. The `terms` field and the key-term asks stay until the end of group 12 (arm 5b, task 12.9, measures them there). ## 3. provider-error-catalogue — Name every provider failure by what it means, and decide what the run does about it (needs group 1)

Groups 2 and 3 may run in parallel after group 1; both touch `TranslationJobImpl` (2.3 removes the batch-format fields, 3.7 adds the run-start log), so group 2 merges first and group 3 rebases onto it.

Makes provider failures classified by the meaning of the reply's text, whatever the HTTP status or word order, with a short reason token that travels with the error. The run then decides by reason: a passage too long for the window is flagged and the book goes on, a model that does not fit in memory asks the person, a stopped or looping reply gets its one more try. Every model call is also counted in one place, with missing figures kept missing. One misread error once paused a night's run for 43 minutes; this group is the fix. No stored shape changes. Rows that need to know which server engine answers (the model-id check, the answer-cap warning, Unsloth, OpenRouter) belong to groups 5 and 8; the token `wrong-model-served` is added here but its check needs the engine and lands in group 5.

## 4. language-data — Function words, name data and rule keys for every language (needs group 1; may run beside group 5: mostly resource files, plus the readers `FunctionWords`, `LanguageRules`, the reviewer classes and `StopWords`)

Ships data and the loaders that read it. A per-language file of short grammar words (function words) lets code tell Russian words left in a Ukrainian text without any list written for one pair; the rule keys let every language declare its name endings, past-tense endings, negation words and conventions as data. No stored shape changes. The behaviour that reads this data is built later (groups 7 and 10); each task here says which requirement its data serves.

- [ ] 4.1 Write the group's acceptance test, the homograph test, and add the `FunctionWords` loader; see the test red. A word both Russian and Ukrainian use (`просто`) must not be counted as a Russian word left in a Ukrainian text, and the test must fail if the Ukrainian file lacks it. → :pipeline
  - **Satisfies:** "Count source-only function words in a target as untranslated residue" (quality-gates; the data side — the check itself is group 10)
  - **Depends on:** group 1
  - **Read:** design D7 "Function words" and "Tests"; `pipeline/glossary/StopWords` (a bundled-list loader to imitate), `pipeline/checks/package-info.java`; the six homographs both languages use are listed in the test below, so nothing else is needed

## 5. provider-engines — know which server answers and build each request from it (needs group 3)

This group makes the server engine a stored, detected fact (Ollama, LM Studio, llama-server, Unsloth, OpenRouter, or a generic
OpenAI-compatible server) and builds every request from the engine's traits. Today one `reasoning_effort` value and one
cap field are sent to every OpenAI-compatible server, which is wrong for most of them. The group also writes ADR-0047
and resets the database once (provider and model-settings shapes change). Transport stays two kinds; the engine only
selects fields, error rows and routes. OpenRouter is detected here but speaks the generic request until group 8.

  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D17 (the group's acceptance test; no requirement of its own)
  - **Depends on:** group 3
  - **Read:** llm: `ChatModelFactoryImpl`, `verify/ProviderVerifierImpl`, `client/openai/OpenAiRequestMapper`, `client/ollama/OllamaReasoning`; tests `llm/client/openai/OpenAiCompatibleClientReasoningTest`, `llm/testing/FakeProviderConfigs`; api: `EnvironmentVariables`; design.md D17
  - **Change:** add `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/ProviderEnginesAcceptanceTest.java`. It uses only the public seams that exist today (`ProviderVerifier.verify` for Test, then `ChatModelFactory` and a draft `ChatRequest`) and a fake `EnvironmentVariables`, so it compiles now and asserts bodies and headers WireMock received.     - `draft_llamaServerWhoseTemplateReadsEnableThinking_sendsKwargFalse`: WireMock answers `GET /health` `{"status":"ok"}` with header `Server: llama.cpp`, `GET /props` `{"build_info":"b11669-620d2225c","role":"model","chat_template":"{% if enable_thinking %}<think>{% endif %}…"}` and 404 for `/api/version`; after Test, a draft with reasoning Off to `qwen3.5-4b` carries `"chat_template_kwargs":{"enable_thinking":false}` (a JSON boolean) and no `reasoning_effort`.
    - `draft_llamaServerGptOssId_sendsReasoningEffortLowAndNoKwarg`: same server, model `gpt-oss-20b`; body has `"reasoning_effort":"low"` and no `chat_template_kwargs`.

Each probe is one GET with a 2 s connect and read timeout, no body, no book text, the key only when the provider has one. Step 0 host rules (`openrouter.ai` incl. `eu.`/`us.` is OpenRouter; `ollama.com` is Ollama with the cloud host label; `api.openai.com`, `api.groq.com`, `api.together.ai`, `api.fireworks.ai`, `api.deepinfra.com` are generic; Anthropic and Gemini hosts are "Not supported yet; reachable through OpenRouter"). Step 1 `root/api/version` then `root/api/tags` then `root/v1/models`: Ollama only when the version body is `{"version":"x.y.z"}`, the tags body is `{"models":[…]}`, no row carries a fake marker (`details.family` `koboldcpp`, `details.format` `sglang`, a digest starting `sha256:sglang`) and, when `/v1/models` returns rows, every row has `owned_by` (an unanswered or 404 `/v1/models` keeps the hit). Step 2 `root/health` (headers read) then `root/props`: llama-server when the header is `Server: llama.cpp` or `/health` is `{"status":"ok"}` with `build_info` matching `^b\d+-`; a `503 Loading model` is llama-server loading; `"role":"router"` sets the router flag. Step 2b `root/api/health` `{"status":"healthy","service":"Unsloth UI Backend"}` is Unsloth. Step 3 `root/api/v0/models` shaped as a list with `compatibility_type` is LM Studio. Step 4 reuses the step-1 `/v1/models` reply when the base path is `root/v1`; the impostor path is therefore 7 GETs at most.

When Ollama answers behind `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE`, the detection carries `offerNativeConnection`; declining sets `nativeOfferDeclined` and nothing else (the engine stays the detected Ollama, `engineChoice` stays empty), and a detection never offers while the flag is set; nothing is ever switched by itself.
    - `effectiveEngine()` is what every later task reads; Detect again clears `engineChoice` and never `nativeOfferDeclined`, so the offer stays declined.

## 6. model-profiles — know each model and handle it by what it is (needs group 5 and the batch-format arm of group 2)

This group reads model names and server facts, builds a model profile (thinking switch, samplers, window, answer cap,
message shaping, quirks, batch size) and makes every request follow it. Today one global setting decides thinking and
sampling for every model, a model without a thinking control gets one anyway, and a model without a system role gets one.
Thinking and Sampling move from Settings › Generation to a per-model override on Settings › Models. The database is reset
once more (per-model overrides and the refused-setting scope are new stored fields).

    - `llm: response/ReasoningScrubber` replaces `ReplySanitizer` (the eval judge of group 1 reads through it from here on, design D24 "Judge"): every known reasoning and turn marker (`<think>…</think>`, `[THINK]…[/THINK]`, harmony channels, gemma channel markers, an opener with no closer), then the `TO_SELF` and `BOXED` filters only where the profile switches them on, then one outer code fence and a leading `json` label, then trim. `response/ReasoningBudget` moves out of `client/openai/` and serves both dialects: a capped reply with blank text, reasoning present (in a field or as an opener with no closer) and finish `length` is sent once more with reasoning off when the model can turn it off, else with the cap multiplied by four inside the context size; the same result again ends as `emptyCompletion` with reason `reasoning-exhausted`.     - Structured rungs `SCHEMA` then `PROMPT_ONLY`, per (model, `PromptName`), stored as `RefusedSetting(name, at, insteadDid, scope)`; a failure belonging to one schema moves that call kind down, a model-wide failure moves them all; rows with `PROMPT_ONLY_START` start low; a call that has reasoning on never carries a schema. ## 7. lemma-book-map — one entry per name with its forms, origins and states (needs groups 4 and 6)

This group turns the glossary from one entry per written form into one entry per base form with its inflected forms. Each
value remembers where it came from (the person, the book, a rule, or the model's likely guess), each target is suggested,
pinned or locked, and a model's guess is shown as "likely" and is never enforced. Today `Велемира` becomes its own name
and an inflected form can flip a name's gender. The database is reset once more. Story shape and the chapter-summaries
switch are group 12's (design D6 places them there); this group's Book Brief work is the policy keys, the declared
conventions, the likely marks and the narrator.

  - **Satisfies:** "Group the inflected forms of a name into one lemma entry" (glossary), "Resolve a form that fits two names by the clause around each mention" (glossary), "Propose names by a deterministic scan when the glossary is empty" (glossary), "Compare glossary terms by one key" (glossary), "Give the model only the terms that occur in the chunk" (glossary; a lemma or any attested form occurs), "Propose new names at the end of each body unit" (translation-pipeline; the unit-end scan groups forms under one base form)

  - **Change:**
    - `pipeline: glossary/NameForms.candidates(form, lang)` (pure): inverts the source file's `nameParadigms` rows (`ending|gender: oblique endings`) so a written form yields the nominative candidates it could belong to; a language with no paradigm gets the identity table. `FrequencyScan` and `CandidateTriage` read the stop words and titles, then group forms whose candidate sets meet; a lemma is accepted when its nominative is attested or two forms agree; the three-use floor counts the whole group; a competition that leaves the target unchanged collapses to the frequent class and sets `lemmaInferred`.
    - Clause evidence (`glossary/ClauseEvidence`): for a form that fits two lemmas, each mention is decided by a gendered past ending on the next word (`pastFemale` / `pastMale`), an address-position word (`addressPositions`) or the oblique ending a preceding function word asks for; a mention with no such evidence is unsure — matched to both entries and casting no gender vote.

## 8. openrouter-provider — make an OpenRouter call correct, not clever (needs group 6; may run beside 7, merged after it)

OpenRouter is detected since group 5 but speaks the generic request. This group adds only what correctness needs: the
reasoning object from its catalog, parameter enforcement, no silent prompt compression, the endpoint's own limits, and
errors that arrive inside a 200. Routing, data-collection, quantisation filters, pinning, `session_id` and free-tier
counters are not built (owner decision). No database reset. Besides bundle keys, 8.4 and group 7 share
`CallRecord`, `CallKindTotals` and `RunSummaryLogger` (7.9 writes the measured seconds per call kind from the ledger),
so group 7 merges first and group 8 rebases onto it before its gate.

- [ ] 8.1 Write the group's acceptance test and see it red. It proves that an OpenRouter reply with HTTP 200 and only an error object is classified as an upstream outage with the reason `provider-unavailable`, not as an unreadable reply. → :llm
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D21 (the group's acceptance test)
  - **Depends on:** group 6
  - **Read:** design.md D21; llm: `client/openai/OpenAiCompatibleClient`, `http/HttpErrorMapper`, `http/UnreadableReply`; api: `FailureReason`
  - **Change:** add `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/OpenRouterAcceptanceTest.java`: a provider on a WireMock address with the engine choice `OPENROUTER` (the host is not openrouter.ai, so the person's "Treat this server as…" choice is used); `POST /api/v1/chat/completions` answers `200` with the body `{"error":{"code":502,"message":"Provider returned error","metadata":{"error_type":"provider_unavailable"}}}`. Method `chat_errorInside200_isUpstreamWithReasonProviderUnavailable` asserts `ErrorCode.upstream` and reason `provider-unavailable`.
  - **Test first:** run it; it fails today (the reply is classified unreadable, not as the outage).
  - **Done when:** the red result is recorded in the task notes.

- [ ] 8.2 Add the OpenRouter traits and request fields, and read the catalog as a facts source. Without the parameter enforcement a provider that cannot honour the schema or the thinking switch is chosen silently, and middle-out compression silently cuts book text. → :llm
  - **Satisfies:** "Ask OpenRouter only for endpoints that honour every parameter sent" (llm-provider), "Turn off OpenRouter's context compression" (llm-provider), "Keep an OpenRouter call within its endpoint's limits" (llm-provider), "Send OpenRouter no routing, data-policy or free-tier controls" (llm-provider), "Send reasoning Off as the engine's own off value" (inference; the OpenRouter case)
  - **Depends on:** 8.1
  - **Excludes:** the error rows (8.3)
  - **Read:** design.md D17, D21; llm: `engine/EngineTraits`, `client/openai/OpenAiRequestMapper`, `dto/OpenAiChatRequest`, `capability/ModelFactsReader`, `client/openai/OpenAiUsage`, `GatedChatModel`; api: `llm/ModelFacts`; the OpenRouter catalog fields `reasoning`, `supported_parameters`, `context_length`, `top_provider.max_completion_tokens` (recorded as fixtures below)
  - **Change:**
    - Research data, first step: write book-neutral fixtures under `modules/llm/src/test/resources/ua/bookloom/llm/openrouter/`: `models.json` (a catalog with four invented entries: one whose `reasoning` lists `none` among efforts, one with a `reasoning` object and no efforts, one where reasoning is mandatory, one with no `reasoning` key; each with `supported_parameters` and `context_length`) and `endpoints.json` (an endpoint with `max_completion_tokens: 4096`, `context_length: 32768`). The shapes come from the OpenRouter catalog notes in the owner-local `10-engines/providers/openrouter.md`; if absent, stop and ask the owner.
    - `EngineTraits` row `OPENROUTER`: cap field `MAX_TOKENS_ONLY`, off switch `REASONING_OBJECT`, reasoning read from `message.reasoning`, the penalty named `repetition_penalty`, `stream:false`, window `HOSTED`. `ModelFactsReader` reads the catalog as a metadata source and the endpoint's limits.
    - Request: `"provider":{"require_parameters":true}` and `"plugins":[{"id":"context-compression","enabled":false}]` on every call; only parameters the endpoint lists in `supported_parameters` are sent; the reasoning object from the catalog (`none` listed gives `{"effort":"none"}`; an object without efforts gives `{"enabled":false}`; mandatory gives the lowest effort; absent is omitted), checked against `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`; the cap is lowered to the endpoint's `max_completion_tokens` when larger and the window is the endpoint's `context_length`, not the model's. Never sent: `data_collection`, a quantisation filter, `order`, `allow_fallbacks`, `session_id`.
  - **Test first:** `OpenRouterRequestTest` (WireMock): `chat_openRouter_sendsRequireParametersAndCompressionOff`, `chat_unsupportedParameter_isNotSent`, `chat_reasoningNoneListed_sendsEffortNone`, `chat_reasoningObjectWithoutEfforts_sendsEnabledFalse`, `chat_reasoningMandatory_sendsLowestEffort`, `chat_reasoningAbsent_sendsNothing`, `chat_capAboveEndpointLimit_isLoweredTo4096`, `facts_window_isTheEndpointContextLengthNotTheModels`, `chat_openRouter_neverSendsRoutingOrDataPolicyFields` (parameterised over the five forbidden names).
  - **Log:** DEBUG the fields chosen and the clamp applied.
  - **Done when:** tests green.

- [ ] 8.3 Read an error that OpenRouter sends inside a 200, and map OpenRouter's own error answers to the catalogue. Today a provider outage hidden in a 200 reads as an unreadable reply, and a refused passage would stop a whole run. → :llm
  - **Satisfies:** "Read an error that OpenRouter sends inside a 200 reply" (llm-provider), "Say why OpenRouter refused a call" (llm-provider), "Ask OpenRouter only for endpoints that honour every parameter sent" (llm-provider; the no-endpoint answer), "Name every classified failure with a reason token" (llm-provider; the OpenRouter tokens), "Map every transport and HTTP outcome to one error code" (llm-provider; the OpenRouter rows)
  - **Depends on:** 8.2
  - **Read:** design.md D18, D21; llm: `http/HttpErrorMapper` (the ordered `ErrorRow` list from group 3), `http/UnreadableReply`, `client/openai/OpenAiCompatibleClient`; api: `FailureReason`; pipeline: `run/PauseDecider` (reason-aware recovery from group 3)
  - **Change:**
    - Before `choices` is read, a `200` body holding only an `error` object, or a choice whose `finish_reason` is `error` (and a stream chunk with a top-level `error`), is read and classified by `error.metadata.error_type`: `provider_unavailable` becomes `ErrorCode.upstream` with reason `provider-unavailable` (backoff retry).
    - New rows for OpenRouter (engine set `{OPENROUTER}`): `402` with `metadata.limit_source` is `ErrorCode.auth` with reason `credits` (the person must act; an in-flight budget honours `Retry-After`); `403` for a content-policy or moderation refusal is `ErrorCode.validation` with reason `blocked` and the run flags that segment and goes on; `408`, `504`, `524` are `timeout`; `502`, `503`, `529` (`provider_*`) are `upstream` with backoff; `429` is `rateLimited`; "no endpoints" with `require_parameters` is `validation` with reason `no-endpoint-for-parameters` and pauses the run for the person. Add the tokens `provider-unavailable`, `credits`, `blocked`, `no-endpoint-for-parameters` to `FailureReason`. The provider's own sentence stays at TRACE.
  - **Test first:** one WireMock fixture per row under `llm/openrouter/` and `OpenRouterErrorRowsTest`: `chat_errorInside200_isUpstreamWithReasonProviderUnavailable` (the acceptance method), `chat_choiceFinishedWithError_isClassifiedByErrorType`, `chat_status402CreditsLimit_isAuthWithCredits`, `chat_status403ContentPolicy_isValidationBlockedAndFlagsTheSegment`, `chat_statuses408_504_524_areTimeout` (parameterised), `chat_statuses502_503_529_areUpstream` (parameterised), `chat_status429_isRateLimited`, `chat_noEndpointForParameters_pausesForThePerson`; `PauseDeciderTest.recovery_credits_isPerson`, `recovery_blocked_flagsAndContinues`.
  - **Log:** WARN lines carry `reason=<token>`; a `blocked` refusal logs the segment id and the token only.
  - **Done when:** tests green; the acceptance method is green.

## 9. model-advice — Quant and language advice for every model (needs groups 6 and 8; may run beside none)

The person sees, before a long run, whether a model's quant or its maker's language list is a risk: a quant chip in
every model picker, identity, quant, language and licence lines on Settings › Models, and lines at Start. It is advice
only: it never blocks, changes no request and stores nothing (design D22). No stored shape changes, so no database reset.

    - `:api` `llm/` records: `QuantAdvice(token, baseType, format, dynamic, imatrix, qat, bitClass, sizeClass, tier, source)`, `QuantTier {EXCELLENT, GOOD, ACCEPTABLE, POOR, UNUSABLE, UNMEASURED, REMOVED}`, `SizeClass {SMALL, MID, LARGE, MOE}`, `LanguageDeclaration(listKind, codes, source)`, `ListKind {COMPLETE, POSITIVE_ONLY, NONE}` (an English-only list is `COMPLETE` with `en`), `LanguageState {DECLARED, NOT_DECLARED, UNKNOWN}`.
    - New package `llm.knowledge` with `package-info.java`; `ModelKnowledgeFiles` (Guice singleton) parses both files once into immutable tables and returns `Result`; a malformed row or a missing data-version line is `ErrorCode.internal`. `:app` bootstrap reads it at start-up, so a broken file stops the start.
  - **Test first:** `ModelKnowledgeFilesTest`: `load_bundledFiles_returnsDataVersion20261028`; `load_rowWithFiveColumnsOfSix_returnsInternalError` (from a `@TempDir` copy); `load_glimmerAndBolt_haveNoneRows`. `AppBootSmokeTest` gains `boot_knowledgeFiles_loaded`.
  - **Log:** INFO `model knowledge loaded dataVersion=… quantRows=… languageRows=…`; ERROR once with the file name and row number on a bad row.
  - **Done when:** the tests are green and `./gradlew :llm:check :app:test` passes.

  - **Satisfies:** "Judge a model's quant from its name and its server's facts" (llm-provider)
  - **Depends on:** 9.2; group 6 (`ModelIdentity.activeB` and its alias facts, 6.3)

  - **Change:** `llm.knowledge.QuantAdvisor.advise(ModelIdentity, @Nullable server facts) → QuantAdvice`:
    - the token and its flags (`UD-`, `_L`/`_XL`, `i1`, QAT) come from the name; base type and format from the server's facts; the facts win when the two disagree on the bit class; MLX and safetensors quants come only from the facts;
    - size class: the effective size from the name first (`e4b` → 4), else the facts' parameter count, else the name's size; `SMALL` ≤ 5B, `MID` > 5–16B, `LARGE` dense above 16B, `MOE` only for a mixture of experts with ≤ 5B active; an unknown active size is `LARGE`; an unknown size gives no tier, only the token and bit class;
    - `UD-` lifts one tier only when the base is 3 bits or fewer, never above UNUSABLE for IQ1, IQ2 and TQ at SMALL and MID; `_L`/`_XL` read their `_M` or base row; `Q4_0` is POOR at SMALL and MID unless the name says QAT;
    - the active size is read from `ModelIdentity.activeB`, whose alias facts 6.3 owns (`gpt-oss:20b` 3.6B so MOE, `gemma4:26b` 4B, `glm-4.7-flash` 3B); `gpt-oss:120b` has none until its vendor card is read, so it is LARGE.

  - **Log:** DEBUG `quant advice model=… token=… base=… sizeClass=… tier=… source=name|facts`; DEBUG when facts and name disagree, with both.

## 10. stage-b-checks-one-repair — Generic checks, code repairs and the one repair (needs groups 4, 6 and 7 and arm 3b; its pure check classes may start beside groups 6 and 7, as design D1 allows, and merge after them)

Every check reads only what a model must fix: code repairs run first, every finding gets a level (block, repair or
note), and a failing segment gets exactly one repair at Balanced and Max, none at Fast but a lost-marks re-draft
(design D11–D14). The directed-fix rounds, the soft-fix call and the listed-foreign-word check are deleted. Stored
findings keep `QaFinding.kind` as a string and do not change the fingerprint; `SegmentRecord` loses `repairRounds`,
which changes it, so the next launch sets the database aside (ADR-0043).

- [ ] 10.1 Write the group's acceptance test, add the `detectors-ru-uk` cases red, and write ADR-0046 before any code. The ADR records the decision the group makes real; the tests prove it. → :pipeline, docs

  - **Depends on:** groups 4, 6, 7; arm 3b's result (group 6)
  - **Read:** design.md D12–D14, D28; `docs/adr/template.md`; `pipeline: SegmentTranslator`, `pipeline: export/ExportServiceImpl`; the `detectors-ru-uk` runner and empty manifest from group 1
  - **Change:**
    - Acceptance, a pipeline e2e test over WireMock, both dialects (`/api/chat` and `/v1/chat/completions`) as a `@ParameterizedTest`:
      (a) `run_headingNumberLost_exportsRestoredNumberWithNoModelCall`: a ru→uk Balanced run of a book that is one Markdown heading line, `# 4. Ночной перевал` (a heading is never sent to the reviewer, which still runs at Balanced until group 14, so no reviewer call is made); the draft reply's target is `Нічний перевал`; export writes `4. Нічний перевал`; WireMock records the draft call and no other.
      (b) `run_russianWordsInDraft_makesExactlyOneRepairCall`: draft `Вони сказали, что ключ у шухляді.`; exactly one repair request follows, recognised by the `[Defects to fix]` heading in its body.
    - `detectors-ru-uk` cases, invented, each written red: a lost leading dialogue dash; a lost heading number; a whole line left in Russian; a residue word `что`; a stress mark `Ла́рин`; and the content-word and mangled-stem cases recorded as expected misses (no finding; only the proofread can catch them).
    - `docs/adr/ADR-0046-three-stages-one-repair-never-worse.md`: Prepare, Translate, Proofread; one repair at Balanced and Max, a lost-marks re-draft only at Fast; the never-worse gate keeps an edit only if no check got worse; export validates and writes with no model call. Status "accepted for the one repair, proposed for the rest"; supersedes ADR-0007 and ADR-0038 when group 14 accepts it fully. Falsifier: paired grading shows more worse than better changed paragraphs. Row added to `docs/adr/README.md`.

- [ ] 10.2 Give every check a level (block, repair or note) on the existing check names, read a finding's level through one reader, and decide each segment by the levels left on it. One source makes "what blocks" the same in the run, the audit and the desk. → :pipeline
  - **Satisfies:** "Give every finding one of three levels: block, repair or note" (quality-gates), "Accept a segment only by the acceptance rule" (quality-gates), "Record each segment's findings for review and repair" (quality-gates), "Block acceptance when a soft check fails outright" (quality-gates), "Let the review mode decide when the run pauses, never what is accepted" (quality-gates), "Fail a segment on a deterministic text defect before any repair or mark" (quality-gates), "Name the exact span in each text finding and bound the text checks" (quality-gates), "Blend the soft-check margins into a documented confidence" (quality-gates), "Let a short echo lower confidence without blocking" (quality-gates), "Leave glossary names out of the echo check" (quality-gates), "Bound the compact reading of a longer short line" (quality-gates), "Keep a usable draft as the machine target when a text check still blocks" (quality-gates), "Treat a refusal or an empty target as a hard gate" (quality-gates), "Treat placeholder integrity, including the order of paired placeholders, as a hard gate" (quality-gates), "Accept a context-matched memory reuse when its checks pass" (quality-gates), "Reuse the translation memory only where its context matches" (translation-pipeline), "Hold a first-person narrator's words to the narrator's gender" (quality-gates), "Keep the gender check soft and per language" (quality-gates), "Read the Ukrainian narrator's past-tense ending" (quality-gates)
  - **Depends on:** 10.1
  - **Read:** design.md D12 "Levels" (no second table), D13 (outcome table); `pipeline: heal/AcceptanceRule`, `heal/SoftFindings`, `heal/BlockingFindings` (reads `isHardGate` today), `qa/QaEvaluator`, `qa/TextCheckGates`, `qa/CheckName` (`hardGate`), `checks/FindingKind`, `checks/UkrainianGenderCheck`, `memory/` translation-memory reuse
  - **Change:** new `qa/CheckLevel {BLOCK, REPAIR, NOTE}`. The existing `qa/CheckName` replaces its `boolean hardGate` with `CheckLevel level`, the level of the check's findings at HIGH severity; a check whose findings differ in level (numbers, names and gender) raises the lower ones at MEDIUM (REPAIR) or LOW (NOTE). One static reader `qa/CheckLevels.of(QaFinding)` turns a stored finding into its level; `BlockingFindings`, `AcceptanceRule` and `heal/SoftFindings` read only it, and `SoftFindings.FIXABLE` is deleted. No second level table exists. A finding's level is derived whenever findings are read, never stored; stored findings keep `QaFinding.kind` as a string, so no new `FindingKind` or `CheckName` constant changes the record fingerprint (design D26: this group's reset comes from `SegmentRecord.repairRounds` going, in 10.7). `AcceptanceRule` accepts when hard gates pass, no soft check failed outright and no BLOCK finding is left; REPAIR findings left become the proofread mark, NOTE findings the audit mark; the confidence orders only. The gender check reads only gender values whose origin is the person or the book (group 7) and raises REPAIR. A memory reuse whose context matches is decided by the same acceptance rule and its checks (no reviewer); if that fails the segment is drafted as usual. No repair call changes yet.

    3. Open the desk: the segment with a residue word names `function-word residue` and the word in its findings list.
    4. Change the apostrophe on the Book Brief: the banner names how many segments it flagged.
    5. Switch to Fast on a fresh copy and translate: no repair line; the export screen names any source-fallback segment.
    6. Export: the heading keeps its number; no stress mark in the file.
  - **Done when:** the gate tail and the hand-check notes are in the merge commit.

## 11. context-builder — One context recipe for every model call (needs groups 10 and 7, group 2's loser removed, arms 4a and 4b; may run beside none)

Every model call gets its context from one builder: a fixed system part per run (role, data sentence, rules chosen once
per book, style sheet, target and pair rules, examples, JSON line) and one last user message with the window (previous
accepted pairs, the focus, the next source), the map items that occur in it and a short reminder (design D8, D9). The
recently-present card, built by code from the book index and the map, takes the rolling summary's who-is-who in the
same task that moves drafts onto the builder, so no draft loses it (design D10, D25). About twenty selector and render
classes are deleted. Arms 5a and 5c run here with the card present; arm 5b runs at the end of group 12. The stored
snapshot changes shape, so the database is set aside.

Then `ContextBuilderAcceptanceTest`, with the pseudo provider and WireMock (both dialects):

## 12. chapter-summaries — Two-to-three-sentence summaries of finished chapters (needs group 11; may run beside none)

Later windows carry short summaries of the earlier chapters, written in the target language after each chapter
finishes, beside the recently-present card that group 11 built; the book-wide rolling summary goes (owner decision D-10,
design D10). Arm 5b (key-term asks) runs last, once the summaries reach block 8. A per-book switch, suggested from the story shape, turns the summaries off. `ChapterSummary` replaces
`RollingSummary` and the brief gains two fields, so the database is set aside.

## 13. stage-c-proofread — the Proofread stage inside every run (needs groups 10, 7, 11, 12 and arms 5a, 5b, 5c; may run beside nothing)

A run gains its third stage, Proofread, at every dial: a term sweep and the deferrals settled by code, then a mark list
built by code, then (Balanced, Max) minimal-edit window calls behind the never-worse gate, and at Max a re-read of every
story paragraph. It replaces Max's backward revision inside the run (design D2, D15), so a mistake found late is fixed
without rewriting text that was already right. The backward-revision classes stay compiled for the export last check
only until group 14 deletes both. The stored shape changes (the proofread verdict kept on a segment, a new deferral reason), so the database is
set aside once.

       Proofread and the title-bar chip follow it.
    3. When Proofread runs, see `window ¶…` rows with before, after and verdict; the run ends Completed only after it.
    4. Open the desk: "Changed by proofread (n)" lists the kept rewrites; Undo proofread restores the text before.
    5. On Names & style set a character's gender after the run, Continue: the run continues at Proofread and the
       segment naming the character is fixed or listed.
    6. Repeat on Fast: no proofread call in "All calls…", the mismatch is listed under "Waiting on a gender".

## 14. remove-old-passes — remove the backward revision, the export last check and the reviewer (needs group 13; arms 7a and 7b run first inside this group because they gate its removals; may run beside nothing)

With the Proofread stage in every run, the older passes that did the same work at a high cost go: Max's backward
revision and the export's last check (export now validates and writes with no model call), and the per-chunk reviewer
as arm 7a decides (default: removed, its deterministic edit guards folded into the never-worse gate). Arms 7a and 7b run
first, right after the acceptance test, because their results gate the removals (design D1, D24). The dial loses its
reviewer and backward-revision fields and their words, the guard cases of the removed tests move to the Proofread and
never-worse tests, and ADR-0046 becomes fully accepted (design D16, D25, D28). The export record changes shape, so the
database is set aside once.

## 15. models-tab-and-timings — timings per engine, the full Models tab, the busy sentence and the name filter (needs groups 5, 6, 8, 9 and 14; may run beside nothing)

Each engine's timings are read where it reports them and shown as a dash where it does not, so a missing figure never
looks like zero. Settings › Models becomes the one place that says who a model is and what its program reports, the old
`ModelAdvice` name rules go, the Providers screen says in plain words why a model check is not started while a run
translates (the existing refusal, reworded), the Projects
screen recommends a model by memory, and every model picker gets a name filter (design D23). No stored shape changes.

## 16. redesign-closing-run — measure the redesigned pipeline against the baseline (needs groups 14, 15 and 12; may run beside nothing)

The closing measurements decide whether the change met its quality goals: the mini suites and `sequence` against the
`79ffede6` baselines, the owner's en→uk book on e4b and once on 26b, and the ru→uk rerun with its own table. Speed is
recorded and reported with no pass rule (owner decision: no time targets). Real books stay on the owner's machine;
only invented-text outputs and the results table are committed (design D24).

- [ ] 16.1 Write the success table with each row's pass rule and record it unfilled (red), so every closing claim has
  a rule fixed before the numbers arrive. → :pipeline (eval), docs
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24
  - **Depends on:** groups 12, 14, 15
  - **Read:** design D24 "Statistics", "Measurements without an arm" and the risks' falsifiers; group 1's baselines;
    `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (`#redesign-arms`, `#15i-baselines`).
  - **Change:** a `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md#redesign-closing-run` section with quality rows and their rules: paired sign test
    p < 0.05 on discordant paragraphs against `79ffede6` (6:0, 8:1, 10:2, 15:4 as the smallest wins); window-injection
    violations 0; mini hard graders 0 failures, soft graders no worse than baseline − max(noise, smallest detectable
    effect); ru→uk rows (0 U+0301 stress marks, 0 letters outside the Ukrainian alphabet such as `ы э ъ ё`, 0 whole
    source lines, one target form per glossary lemma for the top 20 checked by hand, 0 never-worse violations, fewer
    meaning-error paragraphs by the paired sign test plus the absolute rate on 100 random paragraphs with a Wilson 95 %
    interval); speed rows marked "reported, no rule" (prompt-reading share, Max cost against Balanced, whole-book time,
    calls per segment, seconds per call kind, cached share per runtime). Each row reads "not run".
  - **Test first:** none; the table with every row "not run" is the red state
  - **Done when:** the table is committed with every row "not run" (the red state).

- [ ] 16.2 Run the mini suites on e4b against the baselines, so a regression on invented text is caught before the
  long runs. → :pipeline (eval, local), docs
  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24
  - **Depends on:** 16.1
  - **Read:** `scripts/eval-matrix.sh`, `scripts/eval-score.py`, `scripts/probe.sh`, `scripts/eval-models.txt`.
  - **Change:** mini-en and mini-ru, K = 3 with recorded seeds on `gemma4:e4b-mlx`, judge `gemma4:26b` pairwise; diff
    `--paired` against the `79ffede6` baseline; commit the invented-text outputs; fill the mini rows.
  - **Test first:** none; the filled rows are the evidence (an eval run, not code)
  - **Done when:** the rows hold numbers and a pass or fail; a failing hard grader is investigated and fixed in a
    separate task branch before 16.3, or reported to the owner if it needs a decision.

- [ ] 16.3 Run `sequence` paired against `79ffede6`, so the consistency and meaning claims rest on about 320

  - **Satisfies:** enabling work for design D24
  - **Depends on:** 16.2
  - **Read:** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md#redesign-closing-run` (16.1), `scripts/eval-matrix.sh`, `scripts/eval-score.py`
  - **Change:** `sequence` K = 3 on `gemma4:e4b-mlx` at Balanced and Max; the sign test on discordant paragraphs;

numbered hand run of the three stages at each dial on the owner's models, then the project's own documents updated.
There is no separate acceptance test: the gate, the smoke and the hand run are the evidence.

## Workflow follow-up

- After archive, rename the stale reviewer-era scenario titles that the MODIFIED blocks had to keep (OpenSpec refuses a
  MODIFIED block that drops an original scenario name): grep `openspec/specs/*/spec.md` for scenario titles containing
  "reviewer", "directed fix", "repair round" or "consistency" — for example "Fast skips the reviewer", "Max revises after
  the last segment", "The reviewer is announced by kind", "A repair round logs fix", "A draft waits for the reviewer",
  "A directed fix keeps the chunk's rows", "A retry makes no repair rounds", "The report holds counts, flagged segments
  and consistency notes", "The report names no consistency pass" — and give each a title that says what it now tests.
- Rewrite the stale `## Purpose` paragraphs of `openspec/specs/quality-gates/spec.md`,
  `openspec/specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` and `openspec/specs/export/spec.md` (a delta cannot edit them) to name
  the three stages, the check levels with the one repair and the never-worse gate, and an export that validates and
  writes with no model call.
- Edit, as wording only, the untouched requirements whose words still name the old flow: translation-pipeline "Keep a
  segment with nothing to translate as it is" and "Report tokens per second from the recent drafts"; split the long
  legacy statements that this change carried unchanged (app-shell "Show a first-launch state on Projects when none
  exist", about 1,200 characters; export "Write the book in its source format with the target language" and "Report the
  finished file"; resume "Pause on request at the next boundary", "Pause at enabled pause points" and "Pause instead of
  failing when an error stops the job") into one obligation each.
- Then start the next change from `docs/implementation_plan/CHANGE_BACKLOG.md`.
